Line 1305  | // This returns the G_GGX function divided by 2 cos_theta_m, where in practice cos_theta_m is either N.L or N.V.
Line 1306  | // We're dividing this factor off because the overall term we'll end up looks like
Line 1307  | // (see, for example, the first unnumbered equation in B. Burley, "Physically Based Shading at Disney", SIGGRAPH 2012):
Line 1308  | //
Line 1309  | //   F(L.V) D(N.H) G(N.L) G(N.V) / (4 N.L N.V)
Line 1310  | //
Line 1311  | // We're basically regouping this as
Line 1312  | //
Line 1313  | //   F(L.V) D(N.H) [G(N.L)/(2 N.L)] [G(N.V) / (2 N.V)]
Line 1314  | //
Line 1315  | // and thus, this function implements the [G(N.m)/(2 N.m)] part with m = L or V.
Line 1316  | //
Line 1317  | // The contents of the D and G (G1) functions (GGX) are taken from
Line 1318  | // E. Heitz, "Understanding the Masking-Shadowing Function in Microfacet-Based BRDFs", J. Comp. Graph. Tech. 3 (2) (2014).
Line 1319  | // Eqns 71-72 and 85-86 (see also Eqns 43 and 80).
Line 1322  | // Schlick's approximation
Line 1323  | // C. Schlick, "An Inexpensive BRDF Model for Physically-based Rendering", Computer Graphics Forum. 13 (3): 233 (1994)
Line 1324  | // Eq. (19), although see Heitz (2014) the about the problems with his derivation.
Line 1325  | // It nevertheless approximates GGX well with k = alpha/2.
Line 1329  | // float cos2 = cos_theta_m * cos_theta_m;

Line 465   | // These are homogeneous coordinates, as Godot 3 has no Vector4.
Line 466   | // The 1.0 will later become w, the perspective divide.
Line 471   | // If p.d is zero, there is a potential divide by zero ahead.
Line 472   | // This can occur if the test point is exactly on the focal plane
Line 473   | // with a perspective camera matrix (i.e. behind the near plane).
Line 475   | // There are two possibilities here:
Line 476   | // Either the test point is exactly at the origin, in which case the unprojected
Line 477   | // point should theoretically be the center of the viewport, OR
Line 478   | // infinity distance from the center of the viewport.
Line 480   | // We should also handle the case where the test point is CLOSE
Line 481   | // to the focal plane.
Line 482   | // This can cause returned unprojected results near infinity.
Line 483   | // The epsilon chosen here must be small, but still allow for near planes quite close to zero.
Line 485   | // Here we return false and let the calling routine handle this error condition.
Line 487   | // Bodge some kind of result at infinity from the viewport center.
Line 490   | // The viewport size here is irrelevant, we just want a high number
Line 491   | // (representing infinity) but not actually close to infinity to prevent
Line 492   | // knock on bugs if later maths later does something with these values.
Line 493   | // Suffice is for them to be WAY off the main viewport.
Line 521   | // Unproject can fail if the test point is on the camera matrix focal plane
Line 522   | // with a perspective transform.
Line 523   | // In this case, the unprojected point is potentially at infinity from the viewport
Line 524   | // center.
